ParlaMint-AT_2016-05-19-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00130_d7e6766	Mr President of the Federal Republic of Germany! Madam President of the National Council! Dear representatives of the House! I would like to take advantage of the opportunity and explain to you in the next few minutes what my political understanding is and where I believe that we must lead our country together. What I've heard in these conversations is a feeling that you probably know, too, and that is not unknown to you. It is the impression of a standstill that has arisen, and it is a need that goes through our country again a back to fundamentally change things. I have received a lot of encouragement in the last few days, and I have not missed out on what kind of expectation arose. Stroll.]] However, I would also like to start with the expectation, because I am aware that we are living in a country characterized by a variety of institutions characterized by lobbys, which are characterised by a situation of interest, which is also characterized by a clear federalism, and I am of course aware that it is a matter of rolling a stone to the top, under difficult circumstances, on the basis of a difficult challenge, and that it will not be easy for us to do so. It is also logical that we will not be able to do everything we can, that there will be disappointments, that there may be frustration there or there. But what I can promise you is that with every fiber of our will, we will try with our whole passion and every minute of our thinking to move things in the right direction. If we fail, then the right motives will be the reason, so much can I promise you. The second thing I have learned is a remarkable development with which you as a politician – I am, if you want to, more like a newly caught politician – have been confronted for a long time, of course: an incredible shortness of breath, a thunderstorm of appointments, commitments, interview requests, need for conversation. The consequence of this rhythm is a shortness of breath, which is remarkable, and I would like to maintain in this context the following: I think that this is naturally a very bad development and believe that this development, as well as it goes, will have to be withdrawn. I have started my professional life myself as a journalist, as you know, and I know that there are, of course, a lot of need for discussion on this side too, but I think it is useful not to give every microphone a speech, because I am firmly convinced that this country cannot afford a political leadership that does not take time to think. [[Congratulations with SPÖ, ÖVP, Greens and NEOS as well as with representatives of the Stronach team.]] I do not want to leave the impression here on the second day of my term of office, and I do not want to make the impression that we have already solved all the problems or that we know how we will solve all the problems precisely, and I think you should clearly distrust those who are playing this for you. However, what I also believe is that we must pursue a much more focused policy. Politics is often perceived in public perception as such a kind of dog race. It is about who has won, who has enforced in the polls, who has gained a small advantage, who goes off the battlefield with a winner smile; but on all these questions too often the political content has been lost. Political content has been replaced by tactical opportunism, and I think that's exactly what we need to break. We need a clearer emphasis, we need to make clear what we stand for, because one thing I also understood: People don't want compromises, they're burning for principles and attitudes. [[Congratulations with SPÖ, ÖVP, Greens and NEOS as well as with representatives of the Stronach team.]] We will often have to make enough compromises, that is of course, but I do not think we should start our thinking with the compromise. If you look at this in detail, you have to say that this does not actually reflect the realities, because only the working days you have spent here in Parliament, and the agenda and the decisions you have taken, show that in many details this is actually not true. The problem, however, is that in this combination of pragmatic solutions – perhaps even very flat pragmatic solutions – and a rhetorical thunderstorm, which has always been invading on us, has been lost: the understanding of where we want to lead our country. It has become much unclear – and that is what we, I think, all feel. The pictures of the future have been lost. It is no longer clear what our orientation is, it is no longer clear where we want to lead the country, it is no longer clear how to shape our future. In this spiritual vacuum, in the cracks of this vacuum, of course, this building creeps all the more easily the prejudice and the cheap points. I am convinced that we need visions and that we should have the courage to do so, not only from a certain political point of view, but because this is simply and simply a tactical necessity. In 2016, having no visions means that the person who has no visions actually needs a doctor. [[Congratulations with SPÖ, ÖVP, Greens and NEOS as well as with representatives of the Stronach team.]] For our view of the world, for our attitudes we want to argue, and there we will look for the fight. Year of life –, has chosen a great formulation, he has said: People have fears, but it makes no sense to strengthen them in these fears. – This is an access that I would also like to represent here, because I am personally concerned with solving problems, combating the real causes of these fears, but facing them with a positive policy image and a positive world view. [[It was in favour of SPÖ, ÖVP and Greens, as well as Members of the NEOS.]] I want to live in a country in which not only a small minority will have to benefit from the development of prosperity and all the others, where they will remain, as they do on the labour market, on the housing market, and cannot rely on the solidarity of society and on a social security system and network. [[It was in favour of SPÖ, ÖVP and Greens, as well as Members of the NEOS.]] I know that it is common for a statement of government to talk about a lot of policy areas, many things that have been drawn up; it is usually the case that such a declaration of government takes place after the negotiation of a labour agreement. However, I do not want to do this on this occasion, although I know how important it is to discuss topics such as women's policy, European policy and a number of others here. I am convinced, however, that we will have the opportunity to do so in detail in this House. I would like to get to the heart of what is now necessary in the short term. First of all, we have to deal with the question of the loss of confidence and the downside in our country. We see that unemployment is rising, we see that the companies' willingness to invest is very narrow, we have also experienced that the consumption demand and purchasing power of the people in this country has suffered in recent years, we have experienced a period of more than five years of real wage losses. This is an issue that we must devote ourselves to – and we must do so with all the consistency. The crucial lever, as I expect a contribution from all of you, is that we try to turn the mood back in the country, because one I can tell you – coming from the economy – that: The biggest growth brake is the bad mood at the end of the day. The problem associated with it is clear: No economic growth means even more unemployment, even higher debt. – We just can't afford that! That is why my proposal, especially to our partners in the government, is that we jointly develop a project that might be described with the word New Deal. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Singer.]] If you look back in history, you see that this New Deal had several elements, but a very crucial one is that it is about strengthening the investment willingness of private investors, entrepreneurs and entrepreneurs in the short term. In this context, it is essential for us not only to express the willingness to stimulate the economy, but also to expect companies to assume their social responsibility. Jobs, jobs, jobs!, is of course an important formula, but for us it is at least as important that jobs resulting from which people in our country can actually live. [[Congratulations with SPÖ, ÖVP and Greens as well as with representatives of the Stronach team.]] But we cannot rely – and this is logically embedded in Europe in the situation we are living in today – on solving all the problems on our own. It will be one of the main thrusts of our efforts to regain the scope for public investment. We know that, of course, this is only possible in the European area. We need this public investment, we need these scopes for investment in growth and environmental activity, environmental protection. We will use it and carry on these discussions at European level with all the consequence. However, we need not only a short-term plan where the economy needs to be at the heart of the economy – and when I talk about economics, it is a matter of course that not only companies are meant, but at least to the same extent the workers who do the services every day – we must bear in mind that we must not only think in the short term, but also exercise a right to design in relation to our society and the economic system in a medium-term perspective. I want to develop a plan for Austria in 2025 based on the idea of actively shaping the economy and creating a framework in which the economy can develop in our country. In doing so, I think two things are crucial: The first point is that we can better network public and private investment on the basis of clearly defined future patterns. That's crucial. We need the market as far as possible, and we need the state as much as we need. We know, of course, that this idea of free entrepreneurship, which is developed by genius-based products, which are created almost as if by magic hand, is an illusion. We have experienced this on the parade example of the Apple iPhone, you may know the story. Steve Jobs was a brilliant entrepreneur, a great head who in the end understood how to connect the points. In the end, however, he has everything that makes up this phone to thank the fact that it has been promoted and co-developed by public authorities, by public authorities – whether this is the display or the voice recognition system or the GPS system. These are applications that have come from basic research, which has financed the public sector, which has driven public institutions forward and which, ultimately, financed the taxpayers significantly. In the end, it is about developing such models, to say clearly what direction we want where we want to direct our energy and how we want to change the economy in our country. I can tell you, I recently had the opportunity to talk to a number of entrepreneurs in California in Silicon Valley. There are examples of success in Europe, we need not fear, we need no fear, we have the potential to write similar success stories. On this trip to Silicon Valley, in the ten days we spent there with our partners, has once been called the name of a European company, that was the company Herrenknecht. You need to know, this is a German company that produces tunnel construction machines, with Austrian suppliers and Austrian customers. This story should make us confident, because there is something else behind it, namely that we in Europe, that we have incredible strengths in certain sectors in Austria, and to strengthen these strengths must be our goal. This is, for example, the mechanical engineering sector, which is the automotive sector, which is the energy technology, where we have a base, a position of strength that we need to consistently expand. It is about networking public and private investments, it is about connecting companies that go into basic research, it is about companies that ultimately apply them. We must coordinate our universities, we must vote in favour of the entire political framework. In addition to this commitment to the design of our economy in the sense of the people living here, and especially in the sense of increasing employment, I am still talking about a second point. When we look at the major international developments – and we know that the driving forces are globalisation and internationalization and, of course, digitalization – we see that we cannot escape these developments at all. We now have the opportunity to ask ourselves whether we want to wait for these developments to happen to us like a steam roller, or whether it is about us to record this ball and create the conditions in time so that Austria can act successfully in this context. What I mean is this: These developments – digitisation and globalisation – will radically change our entire working world, change the value chain in the economy, and ultimately this means that we will be dealing with significantly less labour in the future in traditional industries, in traditional services sectors. But for us, that means that we have to ask ourselves questions that go very much into the basics and the essentials, namely: How do we distribute work? Finally, how do we want to finance our social security systems, whose funding we need to provide on a much wider basis? And this is also a question of how we focus our education systems on it, because one thing is quite clear: Education policy will be the best social and labour market policy in the future. [[It was in favour of SPÖ and ÖVP, as well as MEPs from Greens and NEOS.]] I have tried to outline in a few minutes a few questions that are not exhaustive at Weitem. And there are, of course, a variety of interesting questions that require political answers. My understanding is that we are not talking about pre-conceptions here, about dogmas and doctrines. My understanding is that there must be an open political discussion to which I would like to invite you personally. Especially in the next few weeks, I would like to deepen the opportunity to have personal conversations with you about your image, about your view, about the things that we need to tackle together. We will do this, however, from a position that marks a positive world view. I think it is a matter of making a positive policy and not serving despair and fears. I am convinced – at least this is my access, which I have experienced in an incredible intensity – that there is no political confusion in this country at all. But there is, of course, a great distance to this capsule policy, which has now clearly removed itself from the people and the real interests, concerns and needs. And if I say that, I mean not only government policy, but also, to the most extent, the responsibility of the opposition for this state. Politics must go out to people, and we must try to activate people to include them in this dialogue. That's for me. I am convinced that our greatest intention, our greatest urge, must be to show people that it is worth getting involved again, because at the end of the day, it is rare that individual people – even here on this government bank – can move the story to make the big difference. In the end, it is the commitment of a variety of individuals that shape history. In that sense, I would like to have a constructive dialogue with you here in the House, but also that we succeed in moving people to re-establish their political commitment. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2016-05-19-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00130_d7e9426	Mr President. Federal Chancellor! (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) High house! There are such moments in life – if you've been dealing with things for a long time, you get to think again and again. And that's how it happened to me recently, when I talked to the Swiss railway passenger transport chief – not the Austrian, but the Swiss railway – about the topic of technology, transport policy and everything Drum and Dran. She meant: The problem in these questions is always that you think too technically. But a door that closes perfectly doesn't make anyone get on a train yet. I believe that this is an essential principle of transport policy: Transport policy is not about technology – technology is an aid – but about man. And I intend to put people at the centre of transport policy, because that is the only way that I believe is successful, ladies and gentlemen. If you do, you realize that transport policy does not mean bringing goods and persons from A to B, and that is what it is all about. Transport policy means thinking about the consequences of what is happening. If you do, you can see that transport policy also has an impact on very, very many other policies. Without interfering with me now, however, transport policy clearly affects health policy, energy policy, labour market policy and many other policies. This is determined by putting people at the centre. That sounds very theoretical now, what does that mean in practice? – In practice, this means that transport policy decides whether people can sleep in the evening or not because either is noise or not. Transport policy means that people have good air that they breathe, or bad air. Transport policy also means: Will more fossil energies be used or will renewable energies be consumed? These are issues that need to be taken into account in transport policy. Transport policy also means: Are people able to reach their workplace cheaply and quickly, or not? – This is also transport policy. The answer, ladies and gentlemen, which I would like to give, is environmental transport policy. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and Greens as well as with representatives of ÖVP, NEOS and Team Stronach.]] The main weapon – forgive me this military expression, but it is appropriate – for environmental transport policy is public transport. Public transport is the answer to many problems that cause transport as a whole. The big challenge is to get people to accept public transport. It doesn't work, just because it's logical. There's no one on the train – perhaps, but not the great crowd – because he thinks it's better. Trains, public transport are then accepted if the offer is appropriate, and there are some criteria that must be voted. This is the pace on the one hand – the pace is important. The branching is important, the attractiveness, a certain coolness that this transport has, is important. The price is important. These are things that we need to achieve, and we need to work on this in passenger transport. But there are also freight transport, and there are also things that need to be addressed in freight transport. We have an excellent export industry in Austria, we have very, very many companies that want to produce and move absolute top products, and then you have to have some flexibility, and you have to discuss the price in freight transport, which is, of course, a major challenge in the economy. That would be the first point, ladies and gentlemen, that is important to me, but there is something else to be considered: It will never succeed in removing the entire traffic from the street, that is illusory. We'll always have road traffic. Now you can think about it: Is it possible to bring this toward electromobility? – Yes, that will probably be possible, but not so fast and not completely. But we'll never get rid of a problem: In my opinion, the roads are still too insecure. We have about twice as many traffic deaths in Austria – don't nail me to the exact number – than in Sweden. ParlaMint-AT_2016-05-19-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00130_d7e14565	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen on the Government Bank! Ladies and gentlemen! Yes, politics must create a future-oriented framework. Of course, it has to face the current and future challenges in order to provide stability and enable long-term planning. This is crucial to enabling creative development, and this is especially true of art and culture. At the same time, however, we need to provide enough space for new things, and I would like to add to what Mr Zinggl has already mentioned this afternoon. There will be nothing else left to us or to talk about it, namely: If we want to allow and promote young and contemporary art, we must certainly also have the courage to shorten some kind of promotion of long existing projects, and ask ourselves whether it is really necessary for each theatre to be promoted to the same extent as in the past. Only in this way will we succeed in giving new minds the chance to establish themselves and thus create new things. The cultural and creative sectors make an indispensable contribution to economic growth, create jobs and contribute significantly to the unmistakableness of our society. Of course, creative work must also be adequately rewarded – just remuneration for creative work. Digitalization has, of course, long since been involved in the arts and culture sector, but there is still a clear lack of the right answers – a modern copyright with a strong copyright law. In particular, it is important to take into account the interest of cultural creators, but also consumers. The goal is and must be that everyone should be in the knowledge society of the 21st century. century can participate in reconciliation of the Internet with copyright, enabling and promoting new, diverse, creative forms of expression. That must be our goal. But I do not understand the promotion of art and culture as a kind of donation of the state. The promotion of art and culture is an investment in the future, in democracy, in our freedom. The maturity and self-confidence of a democracy are also evident in how it deals with art and culture. That is why we must always critically question who is being promoted and whether the funds are fairly distributed, whether we succeed in supporting diversity in the arts and culture scene. Austria currently has a very lively music scene, ranging from folk music to classical music, from pop music to hits. Strengthening the Austrian music industry has been a major concern for me over the past two years – keyword: the ORF's public-law cultural mission. After long negotiations, I succeeded in bringing the ORF into a voluntary quota scheme: At least 15 percent of Austrian music on Ö3 has been available since August 2015. However, we certainly still need to strengthen the Austrian music funds financially, and I am fighting for a music location security law similar to the Film Location Act. This also leads me to the Austrian film. We can be proud of the Austrian film and the successes we celebrate there. We have achieved a number of things, such as the film/TV agreement and after long negotiations also the fixed commitment of the ORF to invest €300 million in the Austrian film industry over the next three years. At the same time, however, we experience a sharp misunderstanding between the sexes in the Austrian film promotion. This question is discussed very hot in the film scene, in the Austrian film industry. Almost 80 percent of the funding continues to go to men. I am already looking forward to presenting to you, Mr President-in-Office of the Council, our new model of how we want and can change this unbearable situation. But it is not only about enabling a wide range of artists, but also about addressing a wide audience, especially a young audience. In my opinion, young people's access to art, to culture, is of central importance. Whoever had contact with art and culture as a child has an open relationship for a lifetime. In addition to educational institutions, regional cultural initiatives in particular have a special role to play. They are an important engine of a lively cultural daily life, in which room for dialogue and tolerance is created. On Tuesday evening we were able to witness here in Parliament how to inspire young people for artistic work; also the Federal Minister has briefly looked by. Together with unaccompanied refugees, actress Hilde Dalik has performed a play and brought us into focus what art can do: Hit bridges, provide stage, promote dialogue and enjoy. And so, too, successful integration policies can look forward to.

ParlaMint-AT_2016-05-19-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00130_d7e14964	Mr President. Dear Members of the Government! Ladies and gentlemen! High house! A modern public infrastructure is based essentially on three pillars, namely a well-developed, modern railway network, fast and stable internet and safe and efficient roads in all parts of the country. In recent years, Austria has made considerable progress in all three areas. First of all, this concerns the railway: The expansion of the environmentally friendly rail has been successful in recent years, and it will continue with vehemence in the coming years. It was, as we know, that around the years 2008/2009 the Austrian rail network was still 80 percent from the period of the monarchy, this investment boost was really very necessary. By 2021 almost €14 billion will be invested in the expansion of the railway; and this will happen with real good reason, because a powerful, modern rail is the backbone for a well-functioning infrastructure and ensures – and above all that is very, very important – hundreds of thousands of jobs. Ladies and gentlemen, not only is investment in the major axes, such as South Railway, West Railway and so on, but also numerous smaller projects have been successfully implemented. Over 100 railway stations have been renovated, revitalized, disability-friendly and modernized in Austria. The increase in the number of passengers at the ÖBB is right. In the previous year alone, ÖBB transported almost 460 million passengers, and the trend is rising. Austria is a railway driver country number one within Europe. The second largest modernisation shift in the area of infrastructure is broadband development. This is an economic and location policy, but also an hour's social policy command. For information only: This year alone, the Republic will support a further €200 million in the Austrian broadband expansion, and the European Union will double this contribution once again. It's already a fact: A quarter of our economic growth is on the ICT account, i.e. on information and communication technologies. There is also a sharp rise in the trend, and in total, the experts expect us, 84 000 new jobs are created by these broadband billions. In addition to rail and broadband, a well-developed road network is the third pillar of a functioning public infrastructure, with the main focus on the gap between road sections and road safety. My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen, unfortunately – and this is not to be concealed either – we had to complain for the first time after 16 years again of road traffic fatalities. However, statistics clearly state that the main causes are the non-use of the belt and the distraction of the tax. 31 percent of all persons killed in road traffic were killed in 2015 due to distraction, and in the case of drivers, mobile phone use on the tax is already accident cause number one. It is all the more important that we decided here in the House last month that the use of the mobile phone on the tax, except as a navigation device with fixed mounting, is explicitly prohibited. Ladies and gentlemen, the area of transport and infrastructure is a big one, but, as I believe, also a particularly exciting and diverse subject area. It is not only about transporting goods and persons, but also – and Our Federal Minister, Mag. Lightenfried, has already said it today – to make a modern, forward-looking health, environmental protection, energy and labour market policy. Minister of Transport Mag. Lightenness, who knows your biography, knows that you have already dealt with the issue of transport and infrastructure at all levels of politics in your work – from the community to the country, to the EU Parliament. You have a wealth of experience. You are an expert in transport policy, and – and this is particularly important – you are really best connected throughout Europe. In this sense, I am looking forward to a good cooperation with you as the Federal Minister and the entire Transport Committee for the best possible benefit of all Austrians. In this sense, I wish you all the best.

ParlaMint-AT_2016-05-19-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00130_d7e15584	Next speaker: Mr Gahr. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2016-05-19-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00130_d7e15605	Mr President. Mr President of the Court of Auditors! (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) We have a lot of reports today. My colleagues, seven in number, will actively comment on the individual reports. All in all, I think, Mrs Moser, we are trying to deal with the reports that are being presented to us as soon as possible. And as far as the session activity is concerned, we do not need to blame ourselves at all. As a whole, we strive to significantly improve the quality and efficiency of our work, and we should continue to work on it in the future. I may briefly comment on the e-medication project. I believe that this is a project that affects all of us, especially in the health field, where it is about efficiency in terms of quality improvement. The Court of Auditors has examined this project e-medication. In the interests of patient safety, it is about questioning the effectiveness of medicinal products given in pharmacies and examining possible interactions and overdoses. This project places some demands on all of us. Participation was voluntary and only with the consent of the patient, and the procedure took place according to clear rules. Overall, this project – I believe that is very important – has spread over various and different regions, over Vienna, the area of Floridsdorf/Donaustadt, over Upper Austria, Wels-Stadt and Wels-Land, and over Tyrol, the districts of Imst, Landeck and Reutte. The Court of Auditors noted that the overall participation was not satisfactory. It was planned that 150 doctors would participate, and there were then almost two thirds. The Court of Auditors has found critically that this project exceeded the total cost of 3.9 million by 24 percent. Overall, there were 16 570 warnings from these surveys where patients reported back and pointed to effects and risks, 110 of which were serious warnings that could have a very massive impact on health treatments. The scientific evaluation of this project has shown that there are also very positive aspects. There are 17 recommendations, 16 addressed to the main association and one to the Ministry of Health. What does the Court of Auditors require? – The Court of Auditors calls for the definition of measurable and verifiable objectives, for greater transparency in terms of costs and plan-ist comparisons, for the framework conditions for evaluations to be defined, and for the project partners to be more transparent, especially as regards the evidence of sub- and performance. The e-medication project has positively assessed 70 percent of the participating doctors, 90 percent of the participating pharmacists and 85 percent of the participating patients. Federal Minister Oberhauser has promised that all recommendations will be implemented. It has succeeded in simplifying the project, it has succeeded in building mutual trust, because there is a certain degree of distrust in the e-card project, and now it is about us implementing this project in the sense of patient safety and realizing it in practice. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2016-05-19-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00130_d7e15787	Madam President. Mr President. I would like to comment briefly on the Court of Auditors' report on the e-Medicine pilot project. This one also has a day-to-day meaning. The electronic health record was launched in December of the previous year for Vienna and for Styria, yesterday the AKH has joined, and at the end of this month, on the 25th, the pilot operation of e-medication in Germany is started. This means that this is a database of medicines and interaction-related medicines, which is also to be stored there. According to the Court of Auditors report, the aim of the review was to assess the organisation, costs, results and procurement aspects of the e-medication pilot project. However, the recipients were the main association and the ELGA GmbH. Main criticisms concern the overrun of the estimated costs. It was total, I think, almost 25 percent to €3.9 million and award errors in the development of the doctor’s software in 2011, for which the main association also imposed fines, which had to be withdrawn after a lawsuit to the Administrative Court in the reporting year 2014. The adoption of the Court of Auditors proposals is in itself very pleasing. In my constituency, in the Donaustadt, a pilot experiment was also carried out, which was not so successful, especially because of that – it was the Social Medical Center East involved and the pharmacies were also involved – because very few patients joined and the participation was too low. However, the Court of Auditors confirms that a total of 16 570 warnings could be presented during the evaluation period, including 110 serious warnings about things that might lead to some death, thus being very serious health impairments. In that respect, I think, one can very well speak of a success of this test. I think e-medicine will continue to be speeded up. The Court of Auditors' price corrections have been taken into account. In the sense of sparingness, this is also implemented. I believe it is very important that this programme is taken up and not only is one concerned with the fear that is being startled here. I believe it is important to detect drug intolerance through the computer and thus save life. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2016-05-19-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00130_d7e15901	Next speaker: Mr. Sieber. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2016-05-19-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00130_d7e15923	Madam President. Mr President of the Court of Auditors! High house! Today we also discuss the report of the Court of Auditors from 2015, which deals with the procurement of the new CONRAD military radio system and also with the counter-actions agreed in connection with the procurement. In 2007, the Ministry of Defence concluded a purchase contract consisting of around 5,000 radios by €76 million. In connection with the procurement, the Ministry of Economic Affairs also concluded a counter-business agreement of over 55 percent Austrian value added of the purchased performance. Ladies and gentlemen! This Court of Auditors report reads like a series of pleas, bad luck and pans to express it very graciously. A first fundamental criticism was made by the Court of Auditors because of the lack of an overall concept for radio equipment equipment in the federal army, which is why the existing diversity of different radio systems in the federal army was not detractable either. The military duty booklet – this term is always found in the report – is based on planning documents, which were only presented as a draft. But precisely from these documents, no concrete need for the system in procurement was clearly detractable. That is to say, there was no clear explanation why exactly this radio system had to be purchased. Nevertheless, the procurement was carried out, with a delivery volume of about €86 million. The Court of Auditors has also identified shortcomings in the award procedure. In the Court's view, the choice of free-lance award in competition was due to the relevant framework conditions. He was critical of the fact that the internal guidelines of the Ministry of Defence, which provided for the application of an obsolete awarding standard, had contradictions with the principles of EU awarding. The Court therefore recommended that the application of all Union-compliant procurement standards be ordered. The Court of Auditors also criticised the resumption of an already excreted bidder in the award procedure, because this was contrary to the fundamental principles of transparency, equal treatment and legal certainty of the bidders. In accordance with the provisions in the supply collection, detailed life cycle cost calculations were to be submitted to the Ministry of Defence within six months of award of the award, even though the Ministry was already bound to the contractor at that time and the cost of life was significantly higher than the purchase costs. All in all, it should be noted that the Ministry of Defence did not have an overview of the budget expenditure for the CONRAD radio system. The follow-up costs for material conservation or for system-related additional procurement were not declared or not fully covered. According to the Court of Auditors surveys, expenditure of around €1.3 million was not taken into account. The direct financial effort increased from € 76 million to at least € 85 million or 13 percent, with the addition of the unannounced additional procurement costs for the acquired system. Finally, in order to mention something positive, it should be said that the agreed value added of 55 percent by Austrian companies is now ensured by the Ministry of Economic Affairs. In all, the Court of Auditors held that: Examining had a lot to criticise, but he also made 36 recommendations. I would like to thank the Court of Auditors for the work they have done, and I hope that we will also learn from this report.

I believe that this is a very negligent approach to science and research in Austria. ParlaMint-AT_2016-05-19-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00130_d7e16645	Madam President. Mr President. High house! On the topic of e-medication: Unfortunately, there were God's planning giants and implementation constraints at work, as we can see from the Court of Auditors' report. A good intention has become a relatively vague project. That's got several reasons. Of course, one wants to have safety and control in medicine. You don't want to harm anyone, and you want to know if medication is causing side effects in patients. Of course, this is good if you take these regulations centrally and you know exactly who gets the medicine. However, if you only do this in a small sector and let it be based only on voluntaryness, you automatically have planning errors inside and can not get a valid result, because you can only generate real patient safety in the medication area if you fully integrate all pharmacies, all patients, all ordinations and all hospitals. That's actually logical, and you don't need to be a doctor. Unfortunately, this project has failed God, but it has cost enough money, about €4 million. But what we have learned from it and what to read, even if the data situation is not very valid, is that the side effect rate is very, very low. We have observed severe side effects only in 0.7 percent of the cases. What's that about? First of all, the doctors seem to work very well. Secondly, Of course, this is also due to the computer system, which is in the ordinations, because there are today in all ordinations or in almost all ordinations computer systems, which are already an e-medication and where the regulations are automatically checked. What is necessary to ensure that the patient does not get an overdose, a missed dose or a secondary medication is to ask the patient exactly what medication he is taking, and that you also know what OTC preparations the patient is committed to once and for all, such as anti-clotting agents such as aspirin. This is one of the most commonly prescribed and self-purchased medications and is almost never questioned. That is quite, very important, because there can be real complications. That is, if we want a good e-medication, then we need a complete control, a complete implementation of the ELGA, and then we need all the data and all the patient's regulations up to Psychopharmaka et cetera. That means, Then we get a glass patient in a way – whether we really want to, we have to think well. There is something else to be read from the e-medication study: We all fall home to a belief in the computer in medicine or in the administration of medicine. One believes these days, with computer and computer, man has become rescueable, and without fail. - That's a mistake. If we want safety in medicine, we have to put on something completely different, namely time. The greatest safety factor for patients is the time the medical staff can devote to the patient concerned. However, this is paradoxically becoming less and less due to the lack of documentation and the documentation obligation that all medical professions today are subject to. Time is getting shorter and less and less. I experience even in the ordination patients telling me about their domestic doctors, about the caregivers, that the doctor is only talking to the patient about the computer. So we have a computer between the patient and the doctor. This is a very bad development. That is why I would like to propose a project for the Court of Auditors: whether one could perhaps check or calculate how much time in the ordinations is devoted to the patient by the doctor. I fear that this time is getting less and less, and yet we are all talking more and more about how much the patient is at the centre. I think we're lying a little bit in the bag, and we have to think about it: away from the total “EDVisation” to a more human-oriented, time-related medicine. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2016-05-19-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00130_d7e17314	Madam President. Mr President of the Court of Auditors! Ladies and gentlemen! A number of points have already been raised by my previous speaker. The test period essentially covered the years 2008 to 2011. The aim of the review was to assess the status of the preventive measures in the framework of public health expenditure on health care. The facts have already been recorded by previous speakers. I may add a major recommendation from the Court of Auditors. The auditors considered that the performance alignment between the system partners was not present, that there was no transparency and coordination and that there was no common strategy. Part of the test report also deals with the review of the mother-child passport, which was introduced 40 years ago. In 2011, one of the audit years, €53 million was spent on this. One of the criticisms of the review was the cost-benefit ratio of the individual studies. However, the responsibility for tasks and financing among the organisations was also assessed negatively. However, there is now a substantial new development at the Mother-Child Pass, as the Minister for Health in the Committee has also told us. The research programme, which has been in existence for 40 years, is being modernised in agreement with the Ministry of Family Affairs. The focus is on more quality, an attractive offer and increased use for the early promotion of children. Ladies and gentlemen, in principle, it must be noted on this report that it was prepared before the current health reform. On the part of the Federal Minister, the Committee has already pointed out that all recommendations have been implemented and that, in particular for the next ten years, some €150 million are available in addition to health care. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2016-05-19-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00130_d7e17714	Madam President. Mr President-in-Office of the Council! High house! In my three-minute speech, I would also like to address the report on gender health in Austria. The aim of the review – which we have heard several times today – was to assess the cross-sectoral distribution of gender health tasks between October and December 2013. The Court of Auditors focused its examination on the Federal Ministry of Health. The examination period essentially covers the years 2009 to 2013. The Court of Auditors issued a total of 13 recommendations to the Federal Ministry of Health. Topics were, for example: Adhesive diseases – especially smoking and alcohol consumption –, cardiovascular diseases, the mammography screening program and the disease management program “Therapy Active” for diabetes. I would now like to take a closer look at the addictions. The Court of Auditors does not see sufficient implementation of gender sensitivity in the national addiction prevention and the alcohol forum. He also criticised the lack of gender and the insufficient realization of the proposals of the alcohol forum. However, I would like to stress positively that the Federal Ministry of Health stated in its opinion that it would implement the remaining recommendations of the alcohol forum. The delays were due to the EU's alcohol policy issues, where a further debate on a future EU alcohol strategy was already being held. With regard to nicotine addiction, the Court of Auditors recommended increasing gender equality, as the proportion of women who consume tobacco has increased considerably. However, according to the Federal Ministry of Health, “both alcohol and tobacco prevalence have always been gender-specific in several national surveys. ” The gender aspect will be taken into account in the preparation of the national addiction prevention strategy and the subject of alcohol addiction will be given an appropriate priority. On the side of the Federal Ministry of Health, the “no smoking phone” is also supported. In addition, campaigns are regularly carried out or promoted to prevent addiction on the one hand, but on the other hand to support the exit from the addiction. In conclusion, it should be noted that since 2011, the Health Ministry has explicitly provided for gender-specific health care in its support priorities. I personally also see this as a matter of fact that institutions, laws and measures must meet both women's and men's concerns. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2016-05-19-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00130_d7e17993	Madam President. Mr President-in-Office of the Council! (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) I have to deal with an audit by the Court of Auditors aimed at the health care system and focus on the mother-child passport. In general, it should be noted that in Austria, expenditure in the health sector is above-average in terms of health care; in 2011, this was at least 58.5 percent, whereas only 1.9 percent of public health expenditure has been spent on health care. In comparison, this percentage in Germany was almost double, namely 3.7 percent. The mother-child pass was made in 1974 with the aim of providing health care for pregnant women and infants up to 62. We have heard this before. This was achieved, and in the years that followed, the programme was extended by additional studies. In the 2008 to 2011 investigation period, the total costs for the mother-child pass increased from 51.3 million to 53.8 million €. Criticism was practised in the fragmentation of competences. The responsibility for tasks and financing was that of three institutions: the Ministry of Health, Social Security and the Family Compensation Fund. This led to expensive changes in the research programme, whose benefits were not always proven. Thus, contrary to the recommendation of an expert body, the internal investigation remained in the programme, resulting in an additional cost of € 2 million per year. Subsequent programme adjustments for 2008 and 2009 amounted to an additional €6.5 million. [[The President-in-Office of the Council shall take over the Presidency.]] It was also criticized that in the past years and decades there has never actually been an overall evaluation of this system. The Court of Auditors is also using its criticism and recommendations. The benefits of the mother-child passport should be reviewed in terms of their cost-benefit value. A quality standard for the implementation of this Mother Child Pass Programme should be adopted and its binding application ensured. In the hope that these points will be implemented, I thank you for the attention and wish you a nice evening.

ParlaMint-AT_2016-05-19-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00130_d7e18060	Mr President. Mr President-in-Office of the Council! High house! On the Court of Auditors' report, a whip through four nuclear medical issues from the doctor's point of view, first of all on the prevention: As the report points out very clearly and as we all know enough, we are doing too little to prevent. I could now speak full-time about the prevention, but I would like to take a look at a subject, namely intestinal cancer prevention. This is a programme that has been implemented in Austria for a number of years, but unfortunately only 12 percent of the population are involved. Ladies and gentlemen, every year we have 5,000 new cases of illness and 3,000 deaths. That means that this is one of the most dangerous, deadly and most common carcinomas. The tactile or the “Good” is that this carcinoma does not cause any complaints for a very long time. But you can discover it very early, with the gut reflection. This programme is too little in the case of Weitem. The only state in which this works half well due to the effort of social security and a very dedicated group of doctors is Vorarlberg. They have managed to raise the study rate to over 20 percent and thus have a reduction in the frequency of colon cancers, i.e. colon cancer frequency, and also a cost savings of € 15 million. I think that we, as politically active people, who are there for the citizens, are all called upon to apply this programme more effectively and to make people afraid of it. This examination, the intestinal reflection, can be performed completely pain-free today, and the diagnosis is at the same time the therapy, because during the examination, equal preliminary stages of cancer, i.e. polyps, can be removed. This is one of the best medical measures that have been developed in recent decades. I can invite you all and ask you to spread it in your circle of friends, friends and parties, in the bodies et cetera. That can save life. We have about 2,000 deaths per year according to calculations. We could avoid these people dying too early. Second point: Hospital care cases, Procuratio cases, as they are specifically called in Vienna. I'm just saying one sentence: As long as we do not have a monocolor financing – i.e. no funding from a single source – and the health insurance companies do not agree to take all health costs, we will not get to grips with this problem. As long as we have separate pots – here the health, here the social one – and the funds are sent back and forth, or they don't want to worry about the other, this problem will not be solved, despite all the good will declarations we have heard from all those involved for many years. Third point: Medical training. The novelle on medical education 2014 is still remembered to all of us. The Court of Auditors report expressed criticism that the responsibility of trainers is not clear. I agree. The training colleagues have no clear concept or clear guidelines on how to train young colleagues. But what is not mentioned in the Court of Auditors' report is the law on medical working time. I think this is highly problematic from the point of view of a long-term hospital director and primary doctor. With a gradual reduction in medical work time, medical training will gradually become worse, because for the doctor, for every specialist, it is extremely important to see as many patients as possible for as long as possible. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Loacker.]] If the working hours of the doctors are reduced, the quality of training will automatically become worse. In Vienna, the ban on overtime is still being added; there is a growing reduction in the time of attendance, and then there is a need to take compensation times, and this is still in young years, if one is able to do good night shifts. Only one comparison number: In the United States, medical work is up to 80 hours a week. With us it is now to be gradually reduced to 48 hours a week. In order to realize that the quality of training will not go along, one does not need to be a medical doctor, but one will unfortunately feel this in one's own body. I am sure that we will have to adapt this law in the foreseeable future – that is, from the EU, too, because from my point of view, this nonsensical regulation comes from Brussels. We will certainly need to find new solutions in Austria, operating agreements et cetera. This will not be the case, especially since the lack of doctors will also strike. A few more words on gender health: I'm right about colleague Lintl, gender mainstreaming is a nonsense. This can be summarized with the word nonsense. [[Abg. Lightenecker: I don't know what...!]] What's not nonsense is gender health when I see it as gender-specific medicine for men and women. There have been women's health specialists for a long time, these are the gynecologists. There are male health specialists, these are the urologists. There are the internalists who intervene on a cross-border basis. I can now tell you why we have such a man-specific medicine: This is due to the studies of the early decades. When medical studies were scientifically rolled out in the 1960s and 1970s, men were confined to voluntary young subjects. These were mostly soldiers or raiders who volunteered for studies. There were no women in there. Why? – The institutes and the pharmaceutical industry were afraid of seeing early pregnancies and of damage to embryonic medicinal products. That's the simple background. This has nothing to do with chauvinism of medicine, but there are simply huge medical data stores with data from men there. Now we have to “research” all this slowly, but surely so to speak, in order to gain new data. But it will not be possible to do that by gender regulation, but it simply takes its five to ten years. The fact that women are more complex in their overall hormonal situation, please, is not an evil chauvinist finding, but it is simply so, and research will therefore become more complex. Of course, we need to focus on it. If women are supposed to be treated worse, this is due to the medical data and not to the fact that doctors or doctors simply see women differently, they want to treat them differently or worse. That's because of the data situation. It's just a scientific fact. If you get smart there, you can get on it a lot and then you can detideologise the debate and simply continue to work on the facts based. - Thank you.

The audits not only show the broad spectrum of audits carried out by the Court of Auditors, but also show that the Court of Auditors' objective is to contribute towards sustainable use of resources and, in particular, to the sustainable performance of tasks. If you look at the tests more closely, they show the need for more efficient use of resources. They show the acute need for action and also the need for basic structural reforms if one wants public funds to actually arrive at those affected. At present, responsibility for tasks, expenditure and financing is diverging in many areas, and the interaction of multiple actors creates an avoidable administrative burden. In addition, the performance of tasks is complex, in transparent and at the same time inefficient. The need for action has also emerged in the context of the debate. Neither the institutions nor the main association nor the Ministry of Social Affairs carried out systematic checks on the uniform enforcement of compensation allowances. In addition, 53 of 61 additional purchases were made without a reasonable check of price adequacy. ParlaMint-AT_2016-05-19-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00130_d7e20030	Mr President. Mr. Minister! With this request, which was originally tabled by us, we decide that the Minister of Integration agrees with the federal states to conclude a 15a agreement providing for uniform integration measures. I think this is very, very important. We currently have many good integration examples in Austria. There are many communities, many cities, even individual federal states, which go a good way here. But I don't think it makes any sense to make a flickering slut. It cannot depend on the state in which I land, on how the integration measures are and which are applied. I think it makes sense for us to have common standards here so that we can know clearly how to take appropriate measures. Above all, we can also learn well from best practice examples that are already implemented in many federal states and municipalities. That is why federal unity is very, very useful. My original request went a little further, namely that asylum seekers should also be included in these integration measures. I believe that we also need to pay great attention to the fact that, in the case of refugees coming to Austria, we are very likely to be there. continue to start much, much earlier, with integration measures, that we offer German courses from the first day and not only on a voluntary basis, as it is now, that we think about an earlier access to the labour market, that we think about competence checks as early as possible. The essential reason for this is quite simple: If we have refugees who have been in the asylum process for three years, who cannot learn German at this time, because there is no opportunity to work on the labour market, and so on and so on, then we will have massive follow-up costs. ParlaMint-AT_2016-05-19-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00130_d7e20670	Mr President. Minister of Justice! High house! Values Advisors! The FPÖ considers that increasing the quality and strengthening the credibility of the final audit is important objectives. We consider all measures that contribute to greater transparency and independence of the auditor to be useful. The FPÖ welcomes the fact that no general extension of external rotation beyond the 10-year term of office has taken place in this draft law to 20 and 24 years respectively. This period – almost half a career of auditors – would have been too long. Many points of the current EU legislation have been introduced in Austria for many years – in some cases significantly excessive. In Austria, rules for internal rotation have existed for more than 10 years, without any relevant EU legislation, which go beyond the current EU requirements in two points. First point: Application not only for PIEs, but also for five-fold-large companies. Second point: Rotation after five years and not after seven years, but currently with a cooling-off period of two years, which – at least for PIEs – needs to be increased to three years due to EU targets. The key to this scheme was that the internal rotation at the time should replace the external rotation that was also discussed. However, the historical reasons are no longer relevant, and we can therefore introduce a lighter regulation here. In view of the external rotation now to be introduced, the personal rotation that has been regulated so far in Austria should therefore be adapted to the minimum requirements of the EU – i.e. an internal rotation after seven years with a three-year Cooling-off period. The amendment to be tabled will ensure this. It should be stressed that small and medium-sized audit firms in particular sometimes have no possibility of internal rotation and are therefore pushed out of the market. An extension of internal rotation therefore favours small and medium-sized audit firms. An instrument that could actually strengthen the independence of the auditor would be the appointment of the auditor before the beginning of the audited financial year and not, as currently foreseen, before the end of the financial year. This would make the respective auditor aware at the time of the ongoing audit whether he has been appointed for the next year or not. This would avoid any possible consideration in the ongoing examination of a possible re-order. Unfortunately, this aspect is not included in the amendment. On the whole, however, we are in agreement with the present draft law as amended by the amendment – which forms the second part of the implementation of EU legislation according to the APAG – and will therefore give our consent to the APRÄG 2016.

ParlaMint-AT_2016-05-19-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00130_d7e20747	Mr President. Federal Minister! High house! Appreciate fellow citizens! “Drum test who binds himself forever” – a famous line from Friedrich Schiller’s poem “The Bell”. I use this line today to stretch the bow to modern economic life, as this quote fits perfectly well for large transactions, mergers of companies or for the conclusion of long-term business relationships. The role of auditors in supporting this is clear: They precisely, primarily from the point of view of balance sheet law, examine whether the principles of proper accounting are also complied with accordingly. With this government proposal on statutory audit law, parts of the EU's statutory audit directive are now transposed into national law concerning corporate law, company law and cooperative law. The aim is to increase the quality of statutory audits and thus also confidence in the capital market. Large costs and red tape for businesses are to be avoided. The importance of the work of auditors and the quality of their work has been clearly demonstrated in the last financial crisis. With the APRÄG 2016, we now want to ensure that the auditors can make their work independent and impartial, but also that the requirements for them are clearer and more predictable. The role of the Audit Committee in Supervisory Boards is also clearly strengthened. I would like to stress the following two measures: To date, auditors of public-interest companies have not been allowed to take a leading position in the audited undertaking for one year after completion of the audit. This temporary ban on activities is now extended to all auditors, on the one hand, and on the other hand extended to two years for public-interest companies. In addition, all statutory auditors involved in the audit should also be included in the ban if they themselves are approved auditors. I think that's a good and right measure. The maximum duration of the audit mandate for auditors and audit firms of undertakings of public interest, the so-called PIEs, is set at 10 It's limited for years. That is, as you know, the external rotation obligation that we have defined in this way. After that, a four-year “cooling phase” follows. We have found a good and balanced way with this limit. I would also like to offer you arguments that speak for long-term cooperation between auditors and companies. According to a European study, initial tests are about 25 percent more expensive than follow-up tests. In the initial audit, the auditor must familiarise himself with the company's accounting and internal control system. On the part of the company, the cost of searching for an appropriate auditor is borne by the company, and there is also an increased commitment of personnel resources, since each time the examiner first checks, he is also to be provided with very basic information. Empirical studies also show that, as the term of office increases, the quality of examination increases rather than decreases. It is therefore good that we have found this long period of time, which also supports these benefits accordingly. My colleague, Mr Groiß, will then put forward an amendment. I am delighted – thank you for the conversations! – that with this amendment, which we have made together with the FPÖ and our coalition partner, we can, in particular, continue to implement those changes that will strengthen small auditors and SMEs. This is a real step towards the economy and the diversity of application procedures. Ladies and gentlemen! We expressly acknowledge all the measures that contribute to further improving transparency and independence in the audit, because quality creates credibility. This strengthens Austria's economic location, and this brings us forward step by step. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2016-05-19-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00130_d7e21126	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, With this proposal of the Auditing Law, drawn up by the Federal Ministry of Justice in close coordination with the Federal Ministry of Finance, those parts of the EU Auditing Directive are now implemented in good time with regard to corporate, corporate and cooperative law, as well as the special arrangements for credit institutions and insurance undertakings. It also lays down detailed rules for implementing the Regulation and exercises national voting rights on individual articles. The basic objective of the directive, in the near future, is to make the requirements for auditors clearer and stricter in order to ensure their independence and impartiality even better than has been the case so far. In addition, the role of the Audit Committee should be strengthened. The new provisions have found a balanced solution that will lead to an increase in the quality of the tests without burdening companies with too high costs or too much bureaucracy. I know you can still do more, you can want more, but I think the consensus that was found here is already a much more viable one. The maximum duration of the audit mandate of an auditor or a company shall be limited to ten years in the case of public-interest undertakings and shall be followed by a four-year cooling period. In addition, there is still a duty to internal personal rotation, even in the case of particularly large subcontractors. take – this is also important. The provision of additional services in addition to the examination is not at all prohibited in the case of public-interest undertakings, so that the examiner does not run the risk of self-examination. Certain tax advisory and evaluation services should remain in addition, but only if they do not pose a risk to the independence of the auditor. The ban, already existing for auditors of public interest companies for one year after the examination, to adopt a leading position in the audited undertaking, will be extended to all auditors, on the one hand, and on the other hand, to two years in the case of statutory audits by public-interest undertakings. These are already marked changes. The additional report to the Audit Committee provided for in the Audit Regulation for public-interest undertakings is also to be returned to large companies. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, If you remember how long we have been discussing this topic for many years, you have to say: With this draft, which is a balanced compromise, which we owe not least to the judicial committee under the leadership of Mr Michaela Steinacker, who has very, very strong and very much tried to find consensus – yes, one must say – it has become possible here in a difficult matter that has been very controversial for many years. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP]] We have a balanced solution that we can put forward. I would therefore ask for your consent. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2016-05-19-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00130_d7e22032	Mr President. Federal Minister! High house! This request concerns the mandatory publication of all final instance judgments of courts. So far, only decisions of the Supreme Court and decisions of other courts have been published in the Federal Legal Information System (RIS). We see several concerns and therefore cannot accept this request. As stated in the application, last-minute court decisions often form the basis for the decision of first instance or other judgments. Therefore, the purpose and purpose of the publication of judgments is that they constitute a guidance function for the legal user. The decisions of the Supreme Court are also written precisely in this consciousness. It is different, however, in some cases in the case of final instance decisions by national courts, Oberlandesgerichten and the Handelsgericht Wien. In accordance with the principle of independence of case law, the views of these courts differ in part, but this lacks the necessary guidance function. - That's one. Of course, anonymizing and providing such decisions is also an appropriate administrative burden. At four Oberlandesgerichten, 18 Landesgerichten and the Handelsgericht Wien we speak of about 40 000 decisions. In order to cope with this quantity, there was a considerable need for more staff to assist the courts in preparing the judgments. The OGH has its own evidence office. In addition, the 40,000 decisions are largely non-directional, which would be essential to the application of the law. Important judgments are already made available by the courts. At present, the legal information system of the Bundes has published 2 726 decisions and legal acts of the OLG. We would therefore create a more bureaucracy with a lower benefit for the general public. In the sense of economics, that cannot be our goal.

But the very important point is that in Austria we have a legal system based on the fact that the judges – and that is good that – can decide freely and independently. It stands in a certain contrast to the so-called Anglo-Saxon, the American system, which is fall-based. You know that, Mr Scherak, too. It is stated in your request: “Legal and jurisdiction always develop along individual cases and individual relevant judgments. For this reason, all the final administrative decisions are of general interest and must be published.” That's just not true for us in Austria. We don't have a case-based system. We do not have this Common Law, as is the case in the English or American countries, but in us, the case law is based, so to speak, on logic and not on experience. That is the very crucial difference. We have the opportunity to take decisions. As a preliminary speaker, Mr Ofenauer has already mentioned the number of decisions that are taken by us in national courts. This is in an order of 40 000 final decisions. So it would be totally impossible to publish all of this. The bureaucratic effort would not benefit in any relation, which it would actually be. To publish all last-minute decisions would also include mass procedures and execution procedures. We know that in the year there are about 1.5 million driving executions or charge executions. I do not want to know how many cost cuts are made, which are then finally decided at last. It is simply the production of a bureaucratic effort which is not necessary, which does not correspond to our legal system, because we do not decide on a case-by-case basis in Austria, but because the judicial bodies make a clear decision on the basis of the law. Keywords: Analogy and so on. It's obviously a different system. That is why, as we now have it, it is quite practical and reasonable. To support this request would therefore also mean supporting a significant bureaucracy would mean creating costs, causing effort. This has nothing to do with transparency, I would like to stress here too. This has nothing to do with transparency, but it is really about dealing with it sparingly and efficiently. And that works. Finally, Mr President, allow me: I would be very pleased if this was my last speech under your presidency today, because I hope you will say good-bye to the Hofburg. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-AT_2016-05-19-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00130_d7e22281	Mr President. Federal Minister! High house! I'll do it briefly. In principle, everything has already been said, just not by me. Maybe very briefly: The application has a certain sex appeal. It is interesting in the point that you publish certain judgments that you can make a bit of a picture in this direction. That is why I was at first very close to saying, okay, we could agree to this, but in a closer look and in the light of the burden of the courts which we already have in the administrative field, I then came to the view that the administrative burden is too high. That is why, because the Stronach team is a party that wants to take a low level of administration and manage it sparingly, we are unfortunately unable to agree to the request. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2016-05-19-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00130_d7e22399	Mr President. We have two requests here. The one concerns the rapid transfer of company book and basic book entries to the Bundesrechenzentrum. Indeed, according to the applicable provisions, for some types of company it is so that the establishment becomes effective with the publication in the company's book. That is, if this transmission takes place only once a day, it sometimes leads to an unnecessary delay. We simply want this transfer to happen several times a day, to happen faster, to happen in real time, then economic operators can start up more flexible and establish their society legally on the same day. Now you have to say: In practice, of course, it is not a great need to show this hurry. There are very different needle tubes in the company's start-up. Den- For example, we know bank confirmations if you start a GmbH in Austria! And I've already founded some and can say: It is sometimes much, much more laborious to get that, and that takes a lot, much longer. Nevertheless, in the EU there is a target for the creation of a business, which is three days. We are a very nice piece away, and it is worth turning on every screw to make the process a little faster. The second request concerns the free provision of basic information from the company book. The company's book, like the base book, is intended to provide everyone with the opportunity to view the data free of charge. My speaking time is already quite over, so the reason is relatively short. We have the year 2016, we live in a digitalized and networked world. These data are not secret, they are machine-readable, they should also be very easily available for everyone. In other countries, this is a long-term standard that you can see, for example, when you look at Poland or Switzerland, where it is using "zefix.ch" – now, please, the directory actually means that – an online database that can be used by anyone around the world. That's how it could be in Austria.
